Now let me analyze the recording points carefully.

**Understanding the loop body:**

In each iteration (when `y != 0`):
```
t -> next = y;     // Link t's next to y
y -> prev = t;      // Link y's prev to t
t = y;              // Move t to y
y = y -> next;      // Move y to y->next
t -> next = u;      // Link t's (now y's) next to u
u -> prev = t;      // Link u's prev to t
if (y) {
    y -> prev = 0;  // If new y is non-null, clear its prev
}
t = u;              // Move t to u
u = u -> next;      // Move u to u->next
```

So in each iteration, a single node `y` is inserted between `t` and `u`. The variable `y` then advances to `y->next`, `t` becomes `u`, and `u` advances to `u->next`.

Wait, let me re-trace:
1. `t -> next = y;` — t's next now points to y
2. `y -> prev = t;` — y's prev now points to t
3. `t = y;` — t moves to y
4. `y = y -> next;` — y moves to y->next (could be null)
5. `t -> next = u;` — t (= old y)'s next points to u
6. `u -> prev = t;` — u's prev points to t (= old y)
7. `if (y) { y -> prev = 0; }` — if new y is non-null, clear its prev
8. `t = u;` — t = old u
9. `u = u -> next;` — u = old u->next

So effectively, one node from y-list is removed and inserted between t and u. After the insertion:
- t points to the node after the inserted one (old u)
- u points to the node after old u
- y points to y->next (one less node in y-list)

- `data_at(z_284_addr, struct list*, z@pre)` — z stack cell
- `dlistrep_shape(z@pre, 0)` — z's list (never modified)

**Sliding items**:
- `data_at(y_287_addr, struct list*, ...)` — y stack cell, value slides: y@pre -> y_316 -> y_324
- `data_at(t_298_addr, struct list*, ...)` — t stack cell, slides: x@pre -> y_309 -> y_320
- `data_at(u_301_addr, struct list*, ...)` — u stack cell, slides: y_309 -> y_320 -> y_338
- x's dlistrep unfolds: the front of x's list grows as y-nodes are inserted

**Key invariants to capture:**

The list starting from x consists of:
1. Nodes originally from x's list (y_309, y_320, y_338, ...)
2. y-nodes inserted between them (y@pre, y_316, ...)

After k iterations:
- t points to the last fully-processed node (the last y-node that was inserted)
- u points to the next x-original node
- y points to the remaining y-list head

The structure is:
- A dllseg from x to u covering the processed prefix (includes original x nodes and inserted y nodes)
- A dlistrep from u covering the remaining original x nodes
- A dlistrep from y covering the remaining original y nodes
- z's list (never touched)

Wait, let me think about this more carefully. After k iterations with y != 0:
- y-nodes have been removed from the y-list and inserted into the x-list
- The x-list grows: x -> (y1) -> (orig_x->next = y_309) -> (y2) -> (orig_x->next->next = y_320) -> ...
- t points to the last processed x-original node (before advancing y)
- But after the insertion completes, t = u_old, so t points to the x-node after the last inserted y-node
- u points to the next x-node

Wait, let me re-read the loop body more carefully:
```
t -> next = y;     // t's next becomes y
y -> prev = t;      // y's prev becomes t
t = y;              // t becomes y
y = y -> next;      // y advances
t -> next = u;      // t (=y_old)'s next becomes u
u -> prev = t;      // u's prev becomes t (=y_old)
if (y) { y -> prev = 0; }  // if new y, clear prev
t = u;              // t becomes u (old)
u = u -> next;      // u advances
```

So after one iteration:
- y_old was inserted between t_old (=x) and u_old (=x->next). The chain is: x -> y_old -> orig_x_next.
- t = u_old (=orig_x_next), u = u_old->next

After two iterations with y != 0:
- Another y-node (y_old->next = y_316) was inserted between t (=orig_x_next) and u (=orig_x_next->next). Chain: x -> y@pre -> orig_x_next -> y_316 -> orig_x_next->next.
- t = u_old (=orig_x_next->next), u = u_old->next

So the general structure:
- The prefix from x up to u consists of: original x-nodes, with y-nodes inserted between them
- t is the node immediately before u in this chain (the last original x-node before u)
- y is the remaining y-list (what hasn't been inserted yet)
- u is the head of the remaining original x-list

The dllseg shape can capture part of the chain. But the exact boundaries are complex because the chain alternates between original x-nodes and inserted y-nodes.

Let me think about what predicates would work...

Option: Use two dllseg shapes
- `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u)` — the segment from x to u (not including u). This covers x through t, including all inserted y-nodes in between.

Actually, let me verify. `dllseg_shape(x, xp, yp, y)` means:
- Segment from x to y (y NOT in segment)
- x->prev = xp (before the segment starts)
- yp is the last node in the segment (yp->next = y)

In our case:
- `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u)` would mean: segment from x to u (u NOT included), x->prev = 0, last node is t, t->next = u

This captures the processed prefix correctly:

Now Step 2: Loop invariant inference. Let me analyze the RPs systematically.

**Analysis:**

The loop inserts nodes from list `y` into list `x`, one at a time, between `t` (processed position) and `u` (next original x-node). Each iteration:
1. Takes the head of y-list, inserts it between t and u
2. Advances t to u, u to u->next, y to y->next

**Diff across RPs:**
- **Frame**: `dlistrep_shape(z, 0)` — never touched
- **Sliding**: x-list segment grows (more nodes inserted), y-list shrinks
- **Structure**: `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u)` covers processed prefix; `dlistrep_shape(u, t)` covers remaining x-nodes; `dlistrep_shape(y, 0)` covers remaining y-nodes

**PROP**: Common across all RPs = `x != 0` (from the `if (x == 0)` guard before the loop)

In dlistrep_shape, t->next as arg[0] means the list starts at the node whose address is t->next. It does NOT create data_at for the field t->next itself — it creates data_at for the fields AT that address.

So dllseg creates: data_at(field_addr(t, next), struct list*, t->next_addr)
And dlistrep creates: data_at(field_addr(t->next_addr, next), ...) * data_at(field_addr(t->next_addr, prev), t)

These are for DIFFERENT addresses:
- field_addr(t, next) = the address of the `next` field inside node t
- field_addr(t->next_addr, next) = the address of the `next` field inside node pointed to by t->next

So NO dup_data_at!

```
        dllseg_shape(head, 0, p->prev, p) *
```

```
 */
```

The `/*@ Inv Assert ... */` format has `&&` between PROP items and then SEP items separated by `*`. The last PROP item is followed by `&&` and then the first SEP item.

My invariant:

OK let me try a MUCH simpler invariant first, just to see if the mechanism works:

```
/*@ Inv Assert 
    dlistrep_shape(x, 0) *

 */
```

If this passes symexec (no fatal errors), it means the invariant mechanism works. If it fails, the issue is with the invariant format, not the logic.

This is TRUE for the modified list — x->next = y@pre, y@pre->prev = x, and dlistrep_shape(y@pre, x). Wait, but dlistrep_shape(y@pre, x) would cover y@pre, u_old, ... which is correct. So dlistrep_shape(x, 0) DOES hold after the first iteration!

So the invariant `dlistrep_shape(x, 0) * dlistrep_shape(y, 0) * dlistrep_shape(z, 0)` might actually work! It's broad enough to cover the modifications and doesn't try to track the insertion point explicitly.

Loop body:
1. `t->next = y`: t = x. From dlistrep_shape(x, 0): data_at(x->next, ...) ✓
2. `y->prev = t`: y is arg[0] of dlistrep_shape(y, 0): data_at(y->prev, ...) ✓
3. `t = y`: stack assignment. t changes from x to y.
4. `y = y->next`: from dlistrep_shape(y, 0): data_at(y->next, ...) ✓
5. `t->next = u`: t = y_old. But is y_old's next accessible? After step 4, y has advanced but y_old's fields (from the original dlistrep_shape(y, 0)) might still be accessible.

Actually, step 3 and 4 re-assign t and y. After `t = y` and `y = y->next`, t points to the old y node. The old y node's fields were exposed by dlistrep_shape(y, 0) unfolding. But after y advances to y->next, the dlistrep_shape(y, 0) predicate now applies to the NEW y. The old y node is NO LONGER part of dlistrep_shape(y, 0) — it was separated from the list.

But the data_at for old y node's next and prev fields are still in the SEP state (from the unfold). So t->next (old y node's next) should still be accessible. ✓

If the invariant REPLACES the entire state, and u is not mentioned, then u's value is lost. The `while(u)` condition would need u's value, and QCP would fail because u is unknown.

So this invariant also won't work because u is missing.

dllseg_shape(z, x, u_prev, u):

- Second dllseg: t to u. t IS in this segment (it's arg[0]). u_prev is the last node before u (= t in this one-node case).
- dlistrep: u to end. u IS in this segment.

- First dllseg: x to x. emp. t_prev = 0.
- Second dllseg: x to x->next. covers x. data_at(x->next, u) * data_at(x->prev, 0). u_prev = x.
- dlistrep: x->next to end. covers from x->next.

All nodes covered exactly once. No overlap. ✓

After one iteration (t=u_old, u=u_new):
- First dllseg: x to u_old. covers x, y@pre. t_prev = y@pre.

- dlistrep: u_new to end. covers from u_new.

All covered. No overlap. ✓

dllseg_shape(x, xp, yp, y) = (x == y && xp == yp && emp) || exists z, data_at(field_addr(x, next), struct list*, z) * data_at(field_addr(x, prev), struct list*, xp) * dllseg_shape(z, x, yp, y)

Each recursive step unfolds one node and creates data_at for its next and prev fields. The recursion continues until x == y (the start reaches the endpoint). At that point, the last node (which was yp) has already been unfolded in a previous step.

- data_at(t->next, u) * data_at(t->prev, t_prev) * dllseg_shape(u, t, t, u)

dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u) where the segment has nodes [x, ..., t]. The expansion:
1. Unfold x: data_at(x->next, n1) * data_at(x->prev, 0) * dllseg_shape(n1, x, t, u)
2. ... (continue for intermediate nodes)
3. Reach t: data_at(t->next, u) * data_at(t->prev, t_prev) * dllseg_shape(u, t, t, u)
4. Base case: dllseg_shape(u, t, t, u) — u == u ✓ AND t == t ✓ → emp. ✓

So YES, dllseg DOES create data_at(t->next, u) when fully unfolded! Step 3 explicitly creates data_at for t->next and t->prev.

The task is complete. Here is the summary:

**File**: `/home/tzh66/loop-invariant-inference/dataset_qcp_infill/System/GlibC/changed_codes/list/multi_append/multi_append.c`
**Result**: `QCP_VERIFIED: NO`

**What was done:**

1. **Step 1 (Symbolic Execution & State Collection)**: Unrolled the `while(u)` loop (depth 3), ran QCP symexec, parsed 3 recording points (RP_pre_loop, RP_iter_2, RP_iter_3) from the log. The symexec on the unrolled file ended with a fatal error at post-loop code, but recording points were captured successfully.

2. **Step 2 (Invariant Inference)**: Analyzed the loop body (inserts y-list nodes between t and u in the x-list), diffed RPs, and constructed the invariant:
   ```
   /*@ Inv Assert x != 0 && u == t->next &&
       dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u) *
       dlistrep_shape(u, t) *
       dlistrep_shape(y, 0) *
       dlistrep_shape(z, 0)
    */
   ```
   - `u == t->next` in PROP was essential: it creates an explicit `data_at(&(t->next), u)` that enables the `t->next = y` heap write. Without it, QCP strategies don't auto-unfold `dllseg_shape` from arg[2] (t) position for field writes.
   - `dllseg_shape(x, 0, t, u)` covers the processed prefix; `dlistrep_shape(u, t)` covers the remaining x-nodes.

3. **Step 3 (QCP Verification)**: After multiple iterations, the invariant passed symbolic execution (`Successfully finished symbolic execution`). However, 6 Coq goals remain Admitted:
   - `entail_wit_1` (entry check)
   - `entail_wit_2_1`, `entail_wit_2_2` (preservation checks)
   - `return_wit_1`, `return_wit_2`, `return_wit_3` (loop exit checks)
   
   The `u == t->next` PROP creates a `data_at` for `t->next` that conflicts with the `dllseg_shape`'s internal representation of the same field, making the entailment proofs manual. Two additional retry attempts (removing `t != 0`, trying broad `dlistrep_shape(x,0)`) did not eliminate the Admitted goals.

**Key finding**: This loop pattern (insertion of y-nodes into x-list with two sliding pointers t and u) requires `u == t->next` in PROP for the heap writes to work, but this inevitably creates `data_at` duplication with the `dllseg_shape` predicate that the strategies cannot fully automate.
